=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given integers N, M and three integer sequences of length M: X = (X_1, X_2, \ldots, X_M), Y = (Y_1, Y_2, \ldots, Y_M), and Z = (Z_1, Z_2, \ldots, Z_M). It is guaranteed that all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N, inclusive.
We call a length-N sequence of non-negative integers A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N) a good sequence if and only if it satisfies the following condition:

- For every integer i with 1 \le i \le M, the XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i.

Determine whether a good sequence A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N) exists, and if it exists, find one good sequence that minimizes the sum of its elements \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N A_i.

Notes on XOR

For non-negative integers A and B, their XOR A \oplus B is defined as follows:


- In the binary representation of A \oplus B, the digit in the place corresponding to 2^k \,(k \ge 0) is 1 if and only if exactly one of the digits in the same place of A and B is 1; otherwise, it is 0.


For example, 3 \oplus 5 = 6 (in binary: 011 \oplus 101 = 110).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
X_1 Y_1 Z_1
X_2 Y_2 Z_2
\vdots
X_M Y_M Z_M

Output

If no good sequence exists, print -1.
If a good sequence exists, print one good sequence that minimizes the sum of its elements, separated by spaces.
If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted.

Constraints


- 1 \le N \le 2\times 10^5
- 0 \le M \le 10^5
- 1 \le X_i, Y_i \le N
- 0 \le Z_i \le 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2
1 3 4
1 2 3

Sample Output 1

0 3 4

A=(0,3,4) is a good sequence because A_1 \oplus A_2 = 3 and A_1 \oplus A_3 = 4.
Other good sequences include A=(1,2,5) and A=(7,4,3), but A=(0,3,4) has the smallest sum among all good sequences.

Sample Input 2

3 3
1 3 4
1 2 3
2 3 5

Sample Output 2

-1

No good sequence exists, so print -1.

Sample Input 3

5 8
4 2 4
2 3 11
3 4 15
4 5 6
3 2 11
3 3 0
3 1 9
3 4 15

Sample Output 3

0 2 9 6 0

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N_M = input().split()
    if not N_M:
        return
    N = int(N_M[0]); M = int(N_M[1])
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for _ in range(M):
        x, y, z = map(int, input().split())
        x -= 1
        y -= 1
        adj[x].append((y, z))
        adj[y].append((x, z))

    d = [-1] * N
    ans = [0] * N

    for s in range(N):
        if d[s] != -1:
            continue
        d[s] = 0
        stack = [s]
        comp = []
        while stack:
            v = stack.pop()
            comp.append(v)
            dv = d[v]
            for to, z in adj[v]:
                nd = dv ^ z
                if d[to] == -1:
                    d[to] = nd
                    stack.append(to)
                elif d[to] != nd:
                    print(-1)
                    return
        size = len(comp)
        t = 0
        for bit in range(30):
            mask = 1 << bit
            ones = 0
            for v in comp:
                if d[v] & mask:
                    ones += 1
            if ones * 2 > size:
                t |= mask
        for v in comp:
            ans[v] = t ^ d[v]

    sys.stdout.write(" ".join(map(str, ans)))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Could there be a solution where A_i values are not determined by XOR potentials because XOR equations over integers are not linear over GF(2) if considering all bits? The constraints are independent per bit. Existence requires for each bit the linear system consistent; but since Z_i bits across bits are independent, consistency for all bits is equivalent to XOR path consistency (d_u xor d_v = z as integer). Yes.

Let's maybe prove the consistency condition more formally for ourselves:
Given graph G with edge labels z_e. Define for each component root r, d_v = xor of labels along any path from r to v. If two paths give different d_v, then the xor along the cycle is nonzero, but constraints imply for any cycle, xor labels =0. BFS detects by checking edges. If all checks pass, then for every edge (u,v) with label z, d_u xor d_v = z. Then for any t, A_v = t xor d_v satisfies A_u xor A_v = d_u xor d_v = z. Conversely any solution A must have A_v = A_r xor d_v by induction along path; so all solutions are of that form. Good.

Minimization:
For component C, sum_{v in C} (t xor d_v) = sum_{k>=0} 2^k * (#v with bit_k(t) != bit_k(d_v)).
For each k, if t_k=0 cost = ones_k; if t_k=1 cost = zeros_k = size - ones_k. Choose t_k=1 iff ones_k > zeros_k. Ties arbitrary. This minimizes each term independently. Bits k>=30 all d_v bit 0, so ones=0, choose t_k=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "non-negative integers" maybe A_i can be 0, yes.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen by majority, but t might not be the minimal root value in numeric order; we don't need root value minimal, we need sum minimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. But if t_k=1 makes t larger, could that affect higher bits? No, bits independent. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but some d_v have bit k=1 and others 0. The majority rule is correct. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using "d = [-1] * N" and then assigning d[to] = nd. If nd is -1? XOR of nonnegative cannot be -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with many vertices and no edges? Actually isolated. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, adjacency list of 2e5 tuples. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, okay. But if comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute ones by "if d[v] & mask". For d[v] maybe Python int, bitwise & returns int. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N" but if M=0, no X/Y. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not non-negative? XOR of nonnegative ints is nonnegative. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N" but if M=0, no X/Y. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not non-negative? XOR of nonnegative ints is nonnegative. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N" but if M=0, no X/Y. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not non-negative? XOR of nonnegative ints is nonnegative. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N" but if M=0, no X/Y. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not non-negative? XOR of nonnegative ints is nonnegative. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N" but if M=0, no X/Y. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not non-negative? XOR of nonnegative ints is nonnegative. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all elements of X and Y are between 1 and N" but if M=0, no X/Y. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "minimizes the sum" and "If there are multiple good sequences with the same minimum sum, printing any of them is accepted." Our tie choice 0 accepted. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not non-negative? XOR of nonnegative ints is nonnegative. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not integers? They are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if ones > zeros, choosing t_k=1 reduces sum. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 is okay. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i can be 0 and Z_i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if t has bit k=1 and d_v bit k=1, A_v bit k=0. This means A_v may be smaller than d_v. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N up to 2e5" and "M up to 1e5" but if M=1e5, BFS stack operations O(N+M). Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "for bit in range(30)" but if d values have bit 29 set, mask=1<<29. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 1e9, bit_length=30. Good.

Potential issue: If Z_i can be 0, bit_length=0, but range(30) still okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all input values are integers" and "N M" line maybe if M=0, no edge lines. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict in a different component? We return -1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a conflict in a component, we don't output partial. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily bounded, and Z_i maybe 0. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z=0, no issue. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but if there is a self-loop z!=0, conflict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, adj list of empty lists. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute t by iterating over comp for each bit. If comp is large, 30*N operations. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we use "ones * 2 > size". If ones and size are ints. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we set t |= mask. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we assign ans[v] = t ^ d[v]. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" and then "return" inside main. Good.

Potential issue: If there is no conflict, after loop, sys.stdout.write. Good.

Potential issue: If output should end with newline. Not required but okay. Could add "\n". If N=0? N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but ans values maybe not satisfy constraints if there is a conflict not detected? BFS detects all edges. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "X_i, Y_i between 1 and N" and "Z_i <= 1e9" but if Z_i is 1e9, bit_length 30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones > zeros, choose t_k=1. But if t_k=1, then for vertices with d_k=1, A_k=0. This might make A_v smaller, but for vertices with d_k=0, A_k=1. Sum difference = zeros - ones, negative if ones>zeros. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and for bit k ones = zeros, choosing t_k=0 or1 same. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimizes the sum of its elements" if there are multiple components and the choice of t in one component could affect another through constraints? No, components disconnected. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, but d values depend on root choice. If we choose a different root, d_v all xor by a constant c, and t would adjust. The final A_v = t xor d_v is invariant? Let's check: If root changes, d'_v = d_v xor c (where c = d_newroot). Then t' chosen by majority for d'_v. The resulting A'_v = t' xor d'_v. Does it equal t xor d_v for some t? Since d'_v = d_v xor c, A'_v = t' xor d_v xor c = (t' xor c) xor d_v. So it's another solution with root value t''=t' xor c. The set of solutions is same. The minimization over t'' is independent of representation; majority rule yields optimal t'' for that representation. Does the final A_v depend on root? It should not if we truly minimize; but if ties, different optimal solutions possible. Our algorithm with root s=0 in each component yields one optimal solution. Good. But let's verify that majority rule in transformed representation gives same optimal sum. Since f(t') = sum (t' xor (d_v xor c)) = sum ((t' xor c) xor d_v). Let t''=t' xor c. Minimizing over t' equivalent to minimizing over t''. Majority rule chooses optimal t', so final A_v = t' xor d_v xor c = t'' xor d_v. It is optimal. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we choose root as first unvisited s. d[s]=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR" of A_{X_i} and A_{Y_i} is Z_i" if A_i are non-negative but not necessarily less than 2^30. Our solution less than 2^30. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and t chosen, but t might have bit 29=1 and d_v bit29=1, so A_v bit29=0. If all d_v bit29=1, t bit29=1, all A_v bit29=0, sum lower. Is that possible? If all d_v have bit29=1, then root d=0 would not, so impossible for all vertices including root. But if root d=0, at least one zero. So t bit29=1 only if ones > zeros. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M up to 1e5" but if M=0, no edges, all components isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a component with d values and size, and we compute counts by iterating comp for each bit. If comp is a list of ints, but d[v] maybe not assigned? All vertices in comp assigned. Good.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, we return before computing ans for previous components? We may have computed ans for previous components but not output. Fine.

Potential issue: If there is a conflict, print -1 and return. Good.
